\section{Further research questions and proposed directions}
\label{cw:sec:questions}

The following questions are proposed continuations. Except for their explicit
connections to the pinned repository, they are not being labeled as previously
published open problems without a separate literature check.

\begin{writenoted}[Relation to the questions of Parts~II and~III]
Seven of the nine questions below continue questions already printed
in this report; they are kept, with their own discussion.
\Cref{cw:q:fixed-space} is the remaining half of \cref{fo:q:invariants}
and contains \cref{fp:q:compact-height}; \cref{cw:q:obstruction} is
Part~II's Research question~3 with the improved bound of
\cref{cw:cor:obstruction}; \cref{cw:q:representatives} refines Part~II's
Research questions~2 and~6; \cref{cw:q:effective} generalizes
\cref{fp:q:effective}; \cref{cw:q:transfinite} is Part~II's Research
question~8; \cref{cw:q:expected} combines Part~II's Research
questions~7 and~9 with \cref{fp:q:expected}; and \cref{cw:q:formal}
is Part~II's Research question~10. \Cref{cw:q:products,cw:q:hierarchies}
are new.
\end{writenoted}

\begin{question}[Which signatures can a fixed space realize?]\label{cw:q:fixed-space}
For a prescribed Hausdorff space $X$ of finite height and fixed alphabet,
characterize
\[
 \{\cwSig(X,c):c:X\to\Sigma\}.
\]
In particular, find local necessary and sufficient conditions for attaining
maximal layer counts at heights at least three.
\end{question}
The present paper classifies a coloring once its signature is known, and
realizes every finite signature on \emph{some} compact space. It does not
classify all colorings on an independently fixed space. The repository's
finite-derived-set capacity theorem is a model for the desired next step.
Nested partition capacities or compatibility of locally attainable suffix
signatures are plausible ingredients, but their exact axioms remain to be
established.

\begin{question}[Optimal obstruction size]\label{cw:q:obstruction}
Determine the least $B(q,m)$ such that every finite colored poset failing an
$m$-layer budget has an induced subposet obstruction of at most $B(q,m)$
vertices. How far can $mq(q-1)^{m-1}$ be reduced? Which antichains force
many distinct witness chains, and when can their vertices be shared?
\end{question}
The proof gives explicit witnesses but makes no attempt to reuse points
between them. Even understanding extremal critical families of reduced
supersequence constraints would improve this bound.

\begin{question}[Minimal representatives]\label{cw:q:representatives}
Given a finite reduced-word downset, determine the minimum number of vertices
in a finite-poset representative, and the minimum number of top-rank points
in a compact representative of optimal height.
\end{question}
The optimal height is settled here, but the disjoint-chain and disjoint-tower
constructions need not optimize other resources. Sharing compatible branches
may save many points or components without changing the signature. The exact
conditions under which sharing introduces unwanted words need a separate
analysis.

\begin{question}[Exact layer complexity of products]\label{cw:q:products}
Express or efficiently bound the SCS length of the product signature from
its two maximal antichains. When is the multiplicative upper bound
$\layers_{X\times Y}\le\layers_X\layers_Y$ attained, and when can it be substantially improved?
\end{question}
The rank law and full signature formula are exact, but shortest master words
must synchronize many possible interleavings. A classification for small
ranks or bounded antichain width would provide concrete test cases.

\begin{question}[Effective topological presentations]\label{cw:q:effective}
For which computable presentations can truncated signatures be extracted
with explicit complexity bounds? Which closure and nonemptiness certificates
can be checked rather than trusted?
\end{question}
Finite posets provide one complete answer. Rank-word towers provide another
finitely presented class. Extending to symbolic descriptions of scattered
spaces requires a precise input language and computational meaning of
closure; otherwise an apparent algorithm can hide a noncomputable oracle.

\begin{question}[Comparison with partition hierarchies]\label{cw:q:hierarchies}
How does finite-depth word equivalence compare with the difference and fine
hierarchies of finite partitions, and with continuous reducibility of those
partitions? Which implications or strict separations hold on common classes
of spaces?
\end{question}
Selivanov's framework \cite{Selivanov} is an appropriate starting point.
The present invariant forgets the locations and multiplicities of closure
witnesses, retaining only which finite patterns occur. Any proposed
identification must respect that loss of information.

\begin{question}[Beyond finite worst-case depth]\label{cw:q:transfinite}
What additional structure on the unbounded signature, or on the sets $F_v$
themselves, controls transfinite open-query games or pointwise finite
strategies with no common finite bound?
\end{question}
When the signature rank is unbounded, the finite admissibility language is
empty. It then cannot distinguish different transfinite behaviors. A
well-founded rank on suitable closure trees might be relevant, but no ordinal
logarithm of the finite layer formula is justified by this paper.

\begin{question}[Expected depth and restricted questions]\label{cw:q:expected}
For a probability measure on $X$, what data beyond the signature determine
minimum expected query depth? Which parts of duality survive when queries
must come from a fixed basis or another restricted family of open sets?
\end{question}
The normal-form proof uses arbitrary finite unions and intersections of
available open sets. A restricted query class may fail that closure property.
Expected-depth optimization also depends on the masses of admissible layers,
which the Boolean occurrence signature deliberately discards.

\begin{question}[A small trusted formal core]\label{cw:q:formal}
Can the operator expansion, diagonal-avoidance lemma, and finite-budget model
theorem be formalized as a compact reusable library, with the topological
results supplied by separate instances?
\end{question}
A successful implementation should make the trusted boundary explicit:
search produces finite certificates, verified word and closed-set lemmas
connect them to semantics, and infinite topology is handled by actual proofs,
not by finite testing alone.

\section{Conclusion}
\label{cw:sec:conclusion}

The key invariant is neither a scalar depth nor a graph of pairwise
accumulations. It is the downset of reduced words surviving iterated color
closures. The finite-union expansion of greedy elimination makes this
language dual to admissible layer words on every topological space. A second,
purely combinatorial separator recovers the language from the full finite
admissibility profile.

This gives an exact classification of finite-depth behavior and a bounded
finite model for each finite budget, even when the original space has no
finite-depth solution. In the Hausdorff setting, longest detected words
measure the least possible representative height. At the first nontrivial
height, the result removes a finite-derived-set restriction from the graph
formula; at higher heights, explicit compact examples show why longer
patterns are essential. For independent products, the same operators give
an exact additive rank law.

The outstanding uniform problem is now more sharply separated from these
settled statements: determine the signatures realizable by colorings of a
\emph{fixed} space. That problem, rather than re-solving the game separately
for each coloring, is a concrete next target for the repository's research
program.

\appendix
\section{Why countable compact Hausdorff spaces are scattered}\label{app:compact}

This appendix supplies the fact used after \cref{cor:allordinals} without
requiring the full ordinal classification of countable compact spaces.

\begin{lemma}
A nonempty compact Hausdorff space with no isolated points has at least
$2^{\aleph_0}$ points.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Construct nonempty open sets $U_s$ indexed by finite binary strings. Start with
the whole space. Inside each $U_s$, select two distinct points; this is possible
because no nonempty open set is a singleton. By regularity, choose nonempty
open neighborhoods $U_{s0},U_{s1}$ with disjoint closures contained in $U_s$.

For each infinite binary string $z$, the compact sets
$\overline{U_{z\upharpoonright n}}$ are nonempty and nested, so their intersection
is nonempty. Choose one point from each intersection. Distinct binary strings
split at some level and hence give points in disjoint closed sets. This produces
an injection of $2^\N$ into the space.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}
Every countable compact Hausdorff space is scattered.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
If a space is not scattered, some nonempty subspace $B$ has no isolated points.
Its closure $F$ has no isolated points either: an isolated point of $F$ would,
by density, belong to $B$ and be isolated in $B$. In a compact Hausdorff space,
$F$ is compact Hausdorff. The preceding lemma would make $F$ uncountable, a
contradiction.
\end{proof}

This proof also shows why no additional set-theoretic hypothesis such as the
continuum hypothesis is needed. Uncountability of $2^\N$ is sufficient.

\section{A proof audit and implementation checklist}

The following points isolate possible sources of off-by-one or topology errors.

\paragraph{Zero questions.}
A constant target has $L=1$, not $L=0$, on a nonempty space. Thus its depth is
$\ceil{\log_2 1}=0$. The empty space is assigned $\sm=0$ separately.

\paragraph{Derivative rank.}
The integer $h$ is the index of the \emph{last nonempty} derivative, not the
height. The height is $h+1$. A convergent sequence has $h=1$ and height $2$.

\paragraph{First closure.}
The general estimate is $F_n\subseteq X^{(n-1)}$ for $n\geq1$, not
$F_n\subseteq X^{(n)}$. The extra first step is exactly what creates the two
oriented lengths $h+1$ and $h+2$ for a parity target.

\paragraph{Hausdorff separation.}
The lower bound with two terminal points uses disjoint open neighborhoods.
The finite-height theorem is therefore stated for Hausdorff spaces. Its proof
is not silently applied to arbitrary $T_0$ posets.

\paragraph{Arbitrary answers.}
The binary-search proof wins on every answer sequence, including one whose
state becomes empty. In the $\omega$-length certification strategy, an
all-no certification path has empty final intersection. There is no hidden
assumption that the Hider fixes a point beforehand.

\paragraph{Infinite sums.}
In the oriented finite-profile formula, an unbounded supremum of integers is
recorded as $\infty$, meaning ``no finite decomposition''. The separate
certification argument is needed to obtain the actual ordinal value $\omega$.

\paragraph{Independent checking.}
The minimax implementation enumerates all open queries. It does not import the
closed-layer answer as its return value. The mathematical proof and the
finite computational check are deliberately distinct.

\section{Certificate and reproduction conventions (Part~II)}
\label{fo:app:certificates}

In \path{data/02-finite-output-strategy_certificate.json} (Part~I's
eight-point certificate is \path{data/strategy_certificate.json}), bit $i$ of
a mask denotes vertex $i$,
with numbering starting at zero. The predecessor mask of a vertex contains
\emph{strict} predecessors. The supplied coloring consists of the chains
$01,02,10,12,20,21$ in that order. The 1-based rank vector is
\[
 (1,2,\ 1,3,\ 2,4,\ 2,3,\ 3,4,\ 3,5).
\]
At an internal tree node, query the upper set encoded by
\texttt{open\_mask}; follow \texttt{yes} or \texttt{no}. A leaf contains the
reported color and its layer number. The word is $01201$, with five layers.

The verifier requires Python 3.10 or later and no third-party packages.
Do not use Python's \texttt{-O} option, which removes assertions. The default
run is the recorded through-five-point run with through-four-point fixed-word
checks. A smaller independent smoke test is
\begin{lstlisting}
python3 code/02-finite-output-verify.py --max-n 3 --brute-n 3 --output <scratch>
\end{lstlisting}
The six-point option is substantially more expensive because both the poset
count and the number of colorings grow quickly. Its availability is not a
claim that a six-point run was performed for this article.

Build the PDF with two runs of
\begin{lstlisting}
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
\end{lstlisting}
The bibliography is embedded below; no BibTeX run, remote assets, or external
figures are required. The archive also includes a Makefile and a source-status
record. Font files are not distributed.

The strategy certificates use zero-based word positions. A node
\texttt{ask\_suffix\_from\_position: j} asks membership in the union of
positions \(j,j+1,\ldots\). The no branch goes to earlier positions and
the yes branch to later positions. Leaf records give a position and its
label. The interpretation as open subsets is supplied by
\cref{fp:thm:profile}; the JSON is not an encoding of an entire infinite
ultrafilter.

The verifier uses Python's arbitrary-precision integers and assertions;
run it without the \texttt{-O} flag. It has no third-party dependencies.

bibliography needs no separate BibTeX step.

\section{Proof dependency and claim audit (Part~IV)}
\label{cw:app:audit}

The following table distinguishes new arguments in this manuscript from
previously available ingredients. ``New here'' describes the contribution
relative to the inspected baseline, not a certified historical priority claim.

\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.26\textwidth}
>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.31\textwidth}
>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.36\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Statement & Dependency & Status and boundary\\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
Statement & Dependency & Status and boundary\\
\midrule
\endhead
Tree--layer equivalence and greedy elimination & Elementary topology and binary trees & Repository baseline; fully reproved in \cref{cw:sec:normal}.\\[4pt]
Closure-word duality, including length-$m$ counterwords & Finite-union operator expansion; diagonal avoidance & New here; arbitrary topology and finite alphabet.\\[4pt]
Finite-rank/SCS reduction & Duality and finiteness of bounded word sets & New all-space reduction; finite-poset SCS interpretation was already in the repository.\\[4pt]
Recovery from admissibility & Greedy forbidden-word separator & New here; requires nonempty finite admissibility language.\\[4pt]
Compact single-word towers & Recursive compactness and suffix neighborhoods & Repository construction; reproved and extended to nonreduced rank words.\\[4pt]
Universal finite-depth classification & Duality, recovery, finite and compact word models & New here; no claim of homeomorphism or continuous equivalence.\\[4pt]
Optimal Hausdorff height & Derivative descent and recovery; tower realization & New here; height is exactly maximum detected reduced-word length.\\[4pt]
Graph formula for $X''=\varnothing$ & Derivative bound; SCS of arcs & Removes the finite-derived-set restriction for fixed colorings. The finite graph optimization is inherited.\\[4pt]
Finite-budget replicas and induced subposet bound & Short counterwords; reduced candidate enumeration & New here; actual induced-subspace conclusion only for finite posets.\\[4pt]
Product rank formula & Rectangular closure identity & New here; applies to independent pair colorings, not arbitrary colorings of a product.\\[4pt]
Matched-space hierarchy examples & Tower rank words and universal reduced-word constant & New examples; shorter pattern occurrence data agree, not all local structures.\\[4pt]
Universal reduced-word constant & Cyclic word and greedy adversary & Repository baseline; not a new extremal-word claim.\\[4pt]
Computational checks & Exhaustive finite enumeration and standard-library code & Executed finite tests; no proof-assistant or infinite-space verification claim.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\normalsize

No theorem of this manuscript assumes an unproved conjecture. The SCS hardness
result is cited only to delimit algorithmic claims; the main proofs do not
depend on it. The descriptive-set-theoretic source provides context and an
explicit further-comparison question, not an imported theorem used without
proof. All nonfinite conclusions are supported by the arguments in the text.

\section{Small worked certificates (Part~IV)}
\label{cw:app:certificates}

\subsection{A failed word and the short obstruction it generates}

Take a three-color signature containing all reduced words of length at most
three, and propose $w=0120$. The coordinatewise-avoiding word $b=1201$
satisfies $b_i\ne w_i$, but is longer than any signature word here and need
not occur. Instead use $v=210$, which does occur and is not embedded in $w$.
Greedy matching of $v$ in the four positions of $w$ matches prefix lengths
$0,0,1,1$. The converse construction of \cref{cw:lem:diagonal} yields
\[
 b=(2,2,1,1),\qquad \red(b)=21.
\]
This is an occurring obstruction of length two: $21\not\preceq0120$.
It contributes a nonempty term $F_{2211}=F_{21}$ to the residual expansion.
The example illustrates that the certificate obtained by the theorem can be
shorter than the originally noticed failed constraint.

\subsection{The need to preserve all admissible words}

Consider a chain with color word $01$ and another with color word $10$.
Both have two layers and one query, but their signatures differ. The first
admits $01$ and the second does not. Their union has maximal antichain
$\{01,10\}$, shortest master word $010$ or $101$, and two queries.
Thus equal component optima do not imply compatibility of optimum words.
This binary phenomenon is already in the repository; the signature theorem
explains it within the same language as arbitrary spaces.

\subsection{A product-rank witness}

Take occurring factor words $010$ and $12$. An asynchronous path gives the
pair word
\[
 (0,1),\ (1,1),\ (0,1),\ (0,2).
\]
Its coordinate reductions are $010$ and $12$, so it occurs in the product.
It has length $3+2-1=4$. The corresponding closed set is exactly
$F^X_{010}\times F^Y_{12}$, by \eqref{cw:eq:rectangle}. No relation between
particular witness points in the two factors is required.
